[LLM] Vulnerable Zebra Zcash node (CVE-2026-44497) — zebrad < 4.4.0 / zebra-script <= 5.0.1 present Exploitation High
Hunts for Zcash Zebra nodes running affected versions (zebrad < 4.4.0 specifically 4.3.1; zebra-script crate <= 5.0.1) which suffer a consensus-divergence bug in transparent sighash hash-type handling. An adversary who broadcasts a transaction with OP_CHECKSIGVERIFY immediately followed by OP_CHECKSIG with an undefined hash type can fork affected nodes off the canonical Zcash chain (Zebra accepts, zcashd rejects). Rationale: Article-specific anchors used: CVE-2026-44497, affected packages rust:zebra-script (<=5.0.1) and rust:zebrad (<4.4.0), affected version 4.3.1, fixed version 4.4.0. Because the exploit is a malformed transparent Zcash transaction broadcast on the network and the article exposes no host-side strings/hashes/log artefacts, the only deployable SOC detection is vulnerable-software presence — patched zebrad >= 4.4.0 fully eliminates the consensus-split risk. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/ZcashFoundation/zebra/security/advisories/GHSA-8m29-fpq5-89jj
ATT&CKT1190 T1499
Data sourcesVulnerabilities.Vulnerabilities Endpoint.Processes
// Vulnerable Zebra presence — preferred path via TVM
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId == "CVE-2026-44497"
   or (SoftwareName has_any ("zebra","zebrad","zebra-script")
       and (SoftwareVersion startswith "4.3."
            or SoftwareVersion startswith "4.2."
            or SoftwareVersion startswith "4.1."
            or SoftwareVersion startswith "4.0."
            or SoftwareVersion startswith "3."
            or SoftwareVersion startswith "5.0."))
| project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, OSVersion, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, RecommendedSecurityUpdateId
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where FileName =~ "zebrad" or FileName =~ "zebrad.exe"
    | summarize LastRun = max(Timestamp), SampleCmd = any(ProcessCommandLine), SampleHash = any(SHA256) by DeviceId
  ) on DeviceId
| order by Timestamp desc

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-44497")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-44497")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] Talos weekly prevalent-malware hash match (Coinminer / Injector / Dropper.Miner) Installation High
Sweep endpoint process, file-write and image-load telemetry for any of the five SHA256s published in the 2026-05-07 Talos Threat Source newsletter as the most prevalent malware files (Win.Worm.Coinminer, W32.Injector, Auto.90B145, MalwareXgenMisc, Win.Dropper.Miner). High-confidence retro-hunt and forward-looking alert because Talos has already attributed each hash to a known family. Rationale: Five SHA256s plus the matching MD5s come straight from the Talos newsletter's 'Most prevalent malware files' block, each with a Talos detection name (Win.Worm.Coinminer::1201, W32.Injector:Gen.21ie.1201, Auto.90B145.282358.in02, W32.Variant:MalwareXgenMisc.29d4.1201, Win.Dropper.Miner::95.sbx.tg). Hashes are non-ambiguous primary IOCs - matching them across process/file/image-load gives an alerting-tier signal with effectively zero false positives. Did not need external corroboration; Talos publishes both the hash and the family name itself.

[LLM] Talos dropper filename pattern d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_<N>_Exe.exe Installation Medium
Hunts processes or file writes whose name matches the very distinctive Talos-observed dropper naming scheme '<MD5-of-parent>_<sequential int>_Exe.exe' - specifically the d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274 family that produced both the W32.Injector (_63_Exe.exe) and Win.Dropper.Miner (_62_Exe.exe) variants in the 2026-05-07 prevalence list. Catches as-yet-unseen sequence numbers (e.g. _64_, _65_) of the same campaign even when a fresh sample's hash hasn't propagated to threat intel yet. Rationale: Both Talos sample filenames d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_62_Exe.exe (a31f222f...) and d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_63_Exe.exe (96fa6a77...) share the same 32-hex-prefix + sequential integer + _Exe.exe suffix - this is a unique enough pattern to act as a hunting anchor for sibling samples whose hashes haven't yet been published. The leading hex string is itself the MD5 of an unspecified parent file, and the _Exe.exe (capital E) suffix is highly atypical for benign software. Marked hunting-tier rather than alerting because we have only two confirmed sequence numbers and want analyst review before auto-blocking new ones.
ATT&CKT1036.005 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let DropperPattern = @"(?i)^d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_\d+_Exe\.exe$";
union isfuzzy=true
(DeviceProcessEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where FileName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, FileName))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, Seq, FolderPath, SHA256, MD5, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, Source="ProcessEvents"),
(DeviceFileEvents
  | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
  | where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed")
  | where FileName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, FileName))
  | project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, Seq, FolderPath, SHA256, MD5, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName, Source="FileEvents")
| order by Timestamp desc
let DropperPattern = @"(?i)d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_\d+_Exe\.exe$";
union isfuzzy=true
(ImProcessCreate
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where TargetProcessName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, TargetProcessName))
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, Seq, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, SHA256, MD5, Source="ImProcessCreate"),
(SecurityEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventID == 4688
  | where NewProcessName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, NewProcessName))
  | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, Seq, CommandLine, ParentProcessName, Source="SecurityEvent_4688"),
(ImFileEvent
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
  | where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed")
  | where TargetFileName matches regex DropperPattern
  | extend Seq = toint(extract(@"_(\d+)_Exe\.exe$", 1, TargetFileName))
  | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFileName, Seq, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256, TargetFileMD5, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, Source="ImFileEvent")
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Talos Dropper Filename Pattern d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_<N>_Exe.exe
id: 4b6e9d12-7c08-4f3a-a4d2-2026050700a2
description: Detects execution of files matching the distinctive Talos-observed dropper naming scheme d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_<seq>_Exe.exe. Two variants (_62_Exe.exe Win.Dropper.Miner, _63_Exe.exe W32.Injector) appeared in the 2026-05-07 Cisco Talos prevalence list; this rule generalises to any sequence number in the same family.
references:
  - https://blog.talosintelligence.com/unplug-your-way-to-better-code/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/07
tags:
  - attack.t1036.005
  - attack.t1204.002
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    Image|re: '(?i)d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_\d+_Exe\.exe$'
  condition: selection
level: medium
source:(windows.sysmon OR windows.security) @Image:(*\\d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_*_Exe.exe OR *\\D4AA3E7010220AD1B458FAC17039C274_*_Exe.exe OR *\\d4aa3e7010220ad1b458fac17039c274_*_EXE.EXE)

DNS tunneling / TXT-heavy domain queries Command & Control Medium
Long subdomain labels + frequent queries to a single 2LD = DNS C2/exfil.
ATT&CKT1071.004 T1048.003
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemotePort == 53 and isnotempty(RemoteUrl)
| extend qlen = strlen(RemoteUrl)
| where qlen > 50
| extend SecondLevelDomain = extract(@"([\w-]+\.[a-zA-Z]{2,})$", 1, RemoteUrl)
| summarize qcount = count(), uniqueSubs = dcount(RemoteUrl), maxLabel = max(qlen)
    by DeviceName, SecondLevelDomain
| where qcount > 100 and uniqueSubs > 20
| order by qcount desc
// ASIM ImDnsActivity normalises Windows DNS, Sysmon EID 22, Linux dnsmasq, etc.
ImDnsActivity
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)
| where DnsQueryTypeName == "TXT"
| summarize Queries = count(), DistinctNames = dcount(DnsQuery)
            by SrcIpAddr, bin(TimeGenerated, 5m)
| where Queries > 200    // tune to TXT-query baseline for environment
| order by Queries desc

| eval qlen=len(query)
| where qlen > 50
| rex field=query "(?<second_level_domain>[\w-]+\.[\w-]+)$"
| stats sum(count) AS qcount, dc(query) AS unique_subs, max(qlen) AS max_label
    by src, second_level_domain
| where qcount > 100 AND unique_subs > 20
| sort - qcount

    where DNS.message_type="QUERY"
    by DNS.src, DNS.query
| `drop_dm_object_name(DNS)`

| where qlen > 50
| rex field=query "(?<second_level_domain>[\w-]+\.[\w-]+)$"
| stats sum(count) AS qcount, dc(query) AS unique_subs, max(qlen) AS max_label
    by src, second_level_domain
| where qcount > 100 AND unique_subs > 20
| sort - qcount

// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["9f1f11a708d393e0a4109ae189bc64f1f3e312653dcf317a2bd406f18ffcc507", "96fa6a7714670823c83099ea01d24d6d3ae8fef027f01a4ddac14f123b1c9974", "90b1456cdbe6bc2779ea0b4736ed9a998a71ae37390331b6ba87e389a49d3d59", "e60ab99da105ee27ee09ea64ed8eb46d8edc92ee37f039dbc3e2bb9f587a33ba", "a31f222fc283227f5e7988d1ad9c0aecd66d58bb7b4d8518ae23e110308dbf91", "2915b3f8b703eb744fc54c81f4a9c67f", "aac3165ece2959f39ff98334618d10d9", "c2efb2dcacba6d3ccc175b6ce1b7ed0a", "dbd8dbecaa80795c135137d69921fdba", "7bdbd180c081fa63ca94f9c22c457376"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Composer post-install/post-update hook spawns Bun runtime or setup-intercom.sh dropper · [LLM] Mini Shai-Hulud payload artefact on disk — router_runtime.js / setup-intercom.sh / known SHA256
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Mini Shai-Hulud C2 beacon — zero.masscan.cloud /v1/telemetry exfiltration endpoint
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Mini Shai-Hulud C2 beacon — zero.masscan.cloud /v1/telemetry exfiltration endpoint Command & Control High
Hunts outbound traffic to the Mini Shai-Hulud campaign C2 endpoint zero.masscan.cloud:443/v1/telemetry, used by the malicious intercom/intercom-php@5.0.2 Composer plugin (and the parallel intercom-client@npm compromise) to exfiltrate harvested AWS/GCP/Azure credentials, .env contents, SSH keys, and CI/CD secrets. The host and path are unique to this campaign — any DNS/HTTPS observation is high-fidelity. Rationale: zero.masscan.cloud:443/v1/telemetry is a campaign-unique C2 endpoint identified by Socket and confirmed across Semgrep / Cloudsmith / StepSecurity write-ups. The host is not used by any legitimate Composer / npm tooling, so any DNS or TLS observation is essentially a true positive. Cross-validated against Socket.dev IOCs and the Wiz Mini Shai-Hulud campaign tracker. Cross-checked against: • https://socket.dev/blog/mini-shai-hulud-packagist-malicious-intercom-php-package-compromise • https://semgrep.dev/blog/2026/malicious-intercom-php-package-spreads-mini-shai-hulud-attack-to-packagist-via-composer-plugin/ • https://www.wiz.io/blog/mini-shai-hulud-supply-chain-sap-npm • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/a-mini-shai-hulud-has-appeared • https://cloudsmith.com/blog/mini-shai-hulud-reaches-packagist-the-intercom-intercom-php-compromise-explained
ATT&CKT1041 T1071.001 T1567

// Mini Shai-Hulud C2 endpoint observed in intercom-php 5.0.2 supply chain compromise
let C2Host = dynamic(["zero.masscan.cloud","masscan.cloud"]);
let C2Path = "/v1/telemetry";
union isfuzzy=true
  ( DeviceNetworkEvents
      | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
      | where RemoteUrl has_any (C2Host) or RemoteUrl has "masscan.cloud"
      | project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId, ActionType,
                RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
                InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
                InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath ),
  ( DeviceEvents
      | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
      | where ActionType == "DnsQueryResponse"
      | extend QueryName = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).QueryName)
      | where QueryName has "masscan.cloud"
      | project Timestamp, DeviceName, DeviceId, ActionType,
                RemoteUrl = QueryName,
                InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
                InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath = "" )
| order by Timestamp asc
// Mini Shai-Hulud C2 — port to Sentinel via ASIM + DNS + W3C IIS proxy logs
let C2Host = dynamic(["zero.masscan.cloud","masscan.cloud"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  ( ImNetworkSession
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where DstHostname has_any (C2Host) or Url has "masscan.cloud"
      | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, DstIpAddr, DstHostname, Url,
                ActingProcessName, ActorUsername ),
  ( ImDnsActivity
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where DnsQuery has "masscan.cloud"
      | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname = SrcHostname, SrcIpAddr,
                DstHostname = DnsQuery, Url = "", ActingProcessName = "", ActorUsername = SrcUsername ),
  ( CommonSecurityLog
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where DestinationHostName has "masscan.cloud" or RequestURL has "masscan.cloud" or RequestURL has "/v1/telemetry"
      | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname = SourceHostName, SrcIpAddr = SourceIP,
                DstIpAddr = DestinationIP, DstHostname = DestinationHostName,
                Url = RequestURL, ActingProcessName = "", ActorUsername = SourceUserName )
| order by TimeGenerated asc

[LLM] Composer post-install/post-update hook spawns Bun runtime or setup-intercom.sh dropper Installation High
Hunts the install-time dropper chain unique to the intercom-php 5.0.2 Composer plugin: a php / composer parent process executing setup-intercom.sh, fetching Bun v1.3.13 from oven-sh/bun GitHub Releases, or spawning the Bun binary on a host that did not previously run it. This is the local execution path of the Mini Shai-Hulud loader and fires only during `composer install` / `composer update` on a compromised dependency. Rationale: Anchors on the three article/Socket IOCs: (a) Composer lifecycle hooks `post-install-cmd` / `post-update-cmd` invoking the plugin entry class `Intercom\ComposerPlugin`, (b) the dropper script names `setup-intercom.sh` and `router_runtime.js`, and (c) the Bun v1.3.13 fetch from `oven-sh/bun`. Parent=php/composer + child=bun/sh+IOC string is high-fidelity because PHP projects almost never spawn Bun via Composer hooks. Cross-checked against: • https://socket.dev/blog/mini-shai-hulud-packagist-malicious-intercom-php-package-compromise • https://semgrep.dev/blog/2026/malicious-intercom-php-package-spreads-mini-shai-hulud-attack-to-packagist-via-composer-plugin/ • https://www.stepsecurity.io/blog/a-mini-shai-hulud-has-appeared • https://www.resecurity.com/blog/article/mini-shai-hulud-a-cross-ecosystem-supply-chain-attack-on-pytorch-lightning-intercom-client
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.004 T1105 T1546.016
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
// Composer post-install/post-update dropper from intercom-php 5.0.2 (Mini Shai-Hulud)
let ComposerParents = dynamic(["php.exe","php","composer.exe","composer","composer.phar"]);
let DropperStrings = dynamic(["setup-intercom.sh","router_runtime.js","bun-v1.3.13","oven-sh/bun","Running Intercom setup script","Intercom\\ComposerPlugin","post-install-cmd","post-update-cmd"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ (ComposerParents))
     or (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("composer install","composer update","composer.phar install","composer.phar update"))
| where FileName in~ ("bun","bun.exe","sh","bash","curl","wget","powershell.exe","pwsh")
      or ProcessCommandLine has_any (DropperStrings)
      or FolderPath has_any (@"/.claude/",@"/.vscode/",@"\.claude\",@"\.vscode\")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage = FolderPath,
          ChildName = FileName,
          ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// Composer dropper chain via ASIM ImProcessCreate + 4688 fallback
let ComposerParents = dynamic(["php.exe","php","composer.exe","composer","composer.phar"]);
let DropperStrings = dynamic(["setup-intercom.sh","router_runtime.js","bun-v1.3.13","oven-sh/bun","Running Intercom setup script",@"Intercom\ComposerPlugin","post-install-cmd","post-update-cmd"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  ( ImProcessCreate
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
      | where (ParentProcessName has_any (ComposerParents)
               or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any ("composer install","composer update"))
      | where TargetProcessName has_any ("bun","sh","bash","curl","wget","powershell","pwsh")
           or TargetProcessCommandLine has_any (DropperStrings)
      | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
                ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine,
                TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, SHA256 ),
  ( SecurityEvent
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where EventID == 4688
      | where Account !endswith "$"
      | where ParentProcessName has_any (ComposerParents)
           or CommandLine has_any ("composer install","composer update")
      | where NewProcessName has_any ("bun.exe","\\sh","\\bash","\\curl.exe","\\wget.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
           or CommandLine has_any (DropperStrings)
      | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname = Computer, ActorUsername = Account,
                ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine = "",
                TargetProcessName = NewProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine = CommandLine, SHA256 = "" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Mini Shai-Hulud payload artefact on disk — router_runtime.js / setup-intercom.sh / known SHA256 Installation High
Hunts file creation of the campaign-specific payload artefacts dropped by intercom-php 5.0.2: setup-intercom.sh (SHA256 832a976d…), router_runtime.js (SHA256 50212a87…), the package zip (66664a49…), and the loader staging paths `.claude/router_runtime.js`, `.vscode/setup.mjs`, `results/results-*.json`. File-name + hash anchors are confirmed by Socket and provide forensic ground truth on any host that resolved the dependency during the 20:53–22:37 UTC window on 2026-04-30. Rationale: All three hashes and both filenames are confirmed by Socket's IOC bundle for the intercom-php 5.0.2 artefact, and the staging paths `.claude/router_runtime.js` / `.vscode/setup.mjs` / `results/results-*.json` are unique to the Mini Shai-Hulud loader. Hash matches are deterministic; filename+path anchors handle re-builds where hashes drift. Cross-checked against: • https://socket.dev/blog/mini-shai-hulud-packagist-malicious-intercom-php-package-compromise • https://security.snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-PHP-INTERCOMINTERCOMPHP-16329836 • https://semgrep.dev/blog/2026/malicious-intercom-php-package-spreads-mini-shai-hulud-attack-to-packagist-via-composer-plugin/ • https://cloudsmith.com/blog/mini-shai-hulud-reaches-packagist-the-intercom-intercom-php-compromise-explained
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.004 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem

let KnownHashes = dynamic([
    "832a976d1a8d54e296e8479aedbd89fa24baa02b8408a78bf06d4d03340881bd",  // setup-intercom.sh
    "50212a875643520353df158196b9b3be4595094125ad8d2d2c48bfd9cb04ce1f",  // router_runtime.js
    "66664a49edbcee0ed0d8365839707916e92d3aa06e7f26f33c9dcc58e5fc1ef3"   // intercom-intercom-php-5.0.2.zip
]);
let KnownNames = dynamic(["setup-intercom.sh","router_runtime.js"]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where SHA256 in (KnownHashes)
    or FileName in~ (KnownNames)
    or FolderPath has_any (@"\.claude\", @"/.claude/", @"\.vscode\", @"/.vscode/")
      and FileName in~ ("router_runtime.js","setup.mjs")
    or (FolderPath has_any (@"\results\", @"/results/") and FileName startswith "results-" and FileName endswith ".json")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType,
          FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc

            - '\powershell.exe'

            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'

falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping

        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/

    - attack.initial-access

    category: process_creation
    product: windows

        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'

            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'

        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,

DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)

ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,

// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version

DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url

         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60

        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd

| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files

ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003

DeviceEvents

| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663

| where Account !endswith "$"

          AccessMask, AccessReason

Actions on Objectives
Likely
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14") or SHA1 in~ ("799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14") or MD5 in~ ("799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14") or SHA1 in~ ("799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14") or MD5 in~ ("799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["799BB5127CA54239D3D4A14367DB3B712012CF14"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Node.js parent spawning OS shell with recon commands (vm2 CVE-2026-44007 post-escape) Exploitation Medium
Hunts the runtime signature of the vm2 NodeVM sandbox escape: a Node.js process spawning a system shell (sh/bash/cmd/powershell) or recon binary (id/whoami/uname/hostname) that mirrors the advisory PoC's `child_process.execSync('id')`. Production Node.js workloads almost never legitimately shell out to identity-recon commands, so the parent=node + child=shell/-c id pattern is a high-fidelity sandbox-escape indicator. Rationale: Article PoC literally calls `require('child_process').execSync('id')` inside the inner NodeVM, so detection targets the resulting parent=node → child=sh -c id (or direct execFile to id/whoami) process tree. Recon-command argument list is anchored to the PoC's `id` plus equivalent Windows/Unix host-recon binaries an attacker would chain after sandbox escape. Tier=alerting because parent and child are both named with strict regex anchors and the cmdline pattern is article-specific. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-8hg8-63c5-gwmx
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1059.003 T1190 T1033
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node", "node.exe", "nodejs", "node22")
| where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","zsh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","pwsh","id","whoami","uname","hostname","systeminfo")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(^|\s|-c\s+|/c\s+)(id|whoami|uname(\s|$)|hostname(\s|$)|systeminfo|cat\s+/etc/(passwd|shadow|hosts)|env(\s|$))"
   or FileName in~ ("id","whoami","uname","hostname","systeminfo")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd   = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage  = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd    = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
let WindowDays = 7d;
(union isfuzzy=true
  (SecurityEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(WindowDays)
    | where EventID == 4688
    | where ParentProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\|/)node(\.exe|js)?$"
    | where NewProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\|/)(sh|bash|dash|zsh|cmd\.exe|powershell\.exe|pwsh\.exe|pwsh|id|whoami|uname|hostname|systeminfo)$"
    | where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(^|\s|-c\s+|/c\s+)(id|whoami|uname(\s|$)|hostname(\s|$)|systeminfo|cat\s+/etc/(passwd|shadow|hosts)|env(\s|$))"
       or NewProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\|/)(id|whoami|uname|hostname|systeminfo)$"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, AccountName=Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine),
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(WindowDays)
    | where ParentProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\|/)node(\.exe|js)?$"
    | where ActingProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\|/)(sh|bash|dash|zsh|cmd\.exe|powershell\.exe|pwsh\.exe|pwsh|id|whoami|uname|hostname|systeminfo)$"
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(^|\s|-c\s+|/c\s+)(id|whoami|uname(\s|$)|hostname(\s|$)|systeminfo|cat\s+/etc/(passwd|shadow|hosts)|env(\s|$))"
       or ActingProcessName matches regex @"(?i)(\\|/)(id|whoami|uname|hostname|systeminfo)$"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer=DvcHostname, AccountName=ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName=ActingProcessName, CommandLine=ActingProcessCommandLine))
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Node.js Parent Spawning Shell or Recon Binary (vm2 CVE-2026-44007 Post-Escape)
id: 8d7c4a02-3f1e-4f9a-9a12-7b1c8e7a44e1
status: experimental
description: Detects Node.js processes spawning a system shell or identity/host-recon binary in the pattern of the vm2 NodeVM sandbox escape PoC, where untrusted sandbox code reaches child_process.execSync to run id/whoami/uname after re-requiring vm2 with require:false bypassed via nesting:true.
references:
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-8hg8-63c5-gwmx

author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/19
tags:
  - attack.t1059.004
  - attack.t1059.003
  - attack.t1190
  - attack.t1033
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '\node.exe'
      - '/node'
      - '/nodejs'
      - '/node22'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/sh'
      - '/bash'
      - '/dash'
      - '/zsh'
      - '\cmd.exe'
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
      - '/pwsh'
      - '/id'
      - '/whoami'
      - '\whoami.exe'
      - '/uname'
      - '/hostname'
      - '\hostname.exe'
      - '\systeminfo.exe'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|re|i: '(^|\s|-c\s+|/c\s+)(id|whoami|uname(\s|$)|hostname(\s|$)|systeminfo|cat\s+/etc/(passwd|shadow|hosts)|env(\s|$))'

  - DevOps tooling (e.g. node-based CI runners) executing system-info collection at build time
  - Health-check scripts in Node.js microservices that legitimately shell out
level: high
source:(windows.sysmon OR linux.auditd) (@ParentImage:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe OR */node OR */nodejs) OR @process.parent.executable.path:(*/node OR */nodejs OR *\\node.exe)) (@Image:(*\\sh.exe OR *\\bash.exe OR *\\cmd.exe OR *\\powershell.exe OR *\\pwsh.exe OR *\\whoami.exe OR *\\hostname.exe OR *\\systeminfo.exe) OR @process.executable.path:(*/sh OR */bash OR */dash OR */zsh OR */id OR */whoami OR */uname OR */hostname)) (@CommandLine:(*" -c id"* OR *" -c whoami"* OR *" -c uname"* OR *" -c hostname"* OR *"/c whoami"* OR *"/c systeminfo"* OR *"/c hostname"* OR *"cat /etc/passwd"*) OR @process.command_line:(*"-c id"* OR *"-c whoami"* OR *"-c uname"* OR *"-c hostname"* OR *"/etc/passwd"*))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/^(node(\.exe|js)?|node22)$/i
| ImageFileName=/(\\|\/)(sh|bash|dash|zsh|cmd\.exe|powershell\.exe|pwsh\.exe|pwsh|id|whoami|uname|hostname|systeminfo(\.exe)?)$/i
| regex("(?i)(^|\\s|-c\\s+|\/c\\s+)(id|whoami|uname(\\s|$)|hostname(\\s|$)|systeminfo|cat\\s+\/etc\/(passwd|shadow|hosts)|env(\\s|$))", field=CommandLine) OR ImageFileName=/(\\|\/)(id|whoami|uname|hostname|systeminfo(\.exe)?)$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

[LLM] Vulnerable vm2 package install discovery via node_modules/vm2/lib/nodevm.js file write Reconnaissance High
Hunts for the presence of vulnerable vm2 npm package installations on managed endpoints/servers by detecting writes to the specific `node_modules/vm2/lib/nodevm.js` file referenced in the advisory's code-walk (the file containing the NESTING_OVERRIDE bug at lines 96-99 and 268-269). Output is the attack-surface inventory the SOC needs to scope CVE-2026-44007 exposure across Node.js workloads. Rationale: Advisory pinpoints `lib/nodevm.js:96-99` (NESTING_OVERRIDE map) and `lib/nodevm.js:268-269` (resolver factory) as the vulnerable code. Filename + path are deterministic across npm/yarn/pnpm installs (all materialize the package at node_modules/vm2/lib/nodevm.js), giving a zero-ambiguity inventory signal. Hunting-tier because every match is a vuln-management ticket, not an active incident. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-8hg8-63c5-gwmx
ATT&CKT1190 T1518
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(90d)
| where FileName =~ "nodevm.js"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\node_modules\vm2\lib", "/node_modules/vm2/lib")
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated", "FileModified", "FileRenamed")
| summarize FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp),
            InstallerProcesses = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName, 10),
            InstallerCmds = make_set(InitiatingProcessCommandLine, 5)
            by DeviceName, FolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| extend AppPath = tostring(split(FolderPath, @"\node_modules\vm2")[0])
| order by LastSeen desc
let WindowDays = 90d;
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(WindowDays)
| where TargetFileName =~ "nodevm.js"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\node_modules\vm2\lib", "/node_modules/vm2/lib")
| where EventType in ("FileCreated", "FileModified")
| summarize FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated),
            InstallerProcesses = make_set(ActingProcessName, 10),
            InstallerCmds = make_set(ActingProcessCommandLine, 5)
            by DvcHostname, TargetFilePath, ActorUsername
| extend AppPath = tostring(split(TargetFilePath, @"\node_modules\vm2")[0])
| order by LastSeen desc

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-44007")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → [LLM] Vulnerable vm2 package present on managed assets (CVE-2026-43999) · Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] vm2 sandbox escape PoC gadget: Module._load('child_process') in Node.js cmdline (CVE-2026-43999) · Article-specific behavioural hunt — [GHSA / CRITICAL] CVE-2026-43999: vm2 has a NodeVM builtin allowlist bypass via
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] node.exe spawning shell with discovery commands (vm2 post-exploit RCE indicator)
[LLM] vm2 sandbox escape PoC gadget: Module._load('child_process') in Node.js cmdline (CVE-2026-43999) Exploitation High
Hunts node.exe processes whose command line carries the exact CVE-2026-43999 escape gadget — `Module._load` plus `child_process` — which only appear together when an attacker is actively chaining the vm2 builtin-allowlist bypass to load the blocked `child_process` module from the host context. Catches `-e` inline exploitation and developer/red-team PoC reuse. Rationale: Uses the exact PoC strings from the GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8 advisory body — `Module._load`, `child_process`, `require('module')` — which only co-occur in the documented sandbox-escape chain (steps 5-7 of the execution chain in the article). Co-occurrence of both tokens in a node.exe command line is high-fidelity because legitimate Node code rarely calls private `_load` directly. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1068
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where FileName in~ ("node.exe","node") or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
| where (ProcessCommandLine has "Module._load" and ProcessCommandLine has "child_process")
   or (ProcessCommandLine has "require('module')" and ProcessCommandLine has "_load")
   or (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "Module._load" and InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "child_process")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\node.exe" or ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe"
| where (CommandLine has "Module._load" and CommandLine has "child_process")
   or (CommandLine has "require('module')" and CommandLine has "_load")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, ParentProcessName, CommandLine, ProcessId, SubjectUserName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: vm2 NodeVM Sandbox Escape Gadget (CVE-2026-43999)
id: 1f43c0a7-9e3d-4cb1-8a2f-7c5a3b9d2e10
description: Detects command-line invocation of the vm2 sandbox-escape gadget that uses module._load to bypass the NodeVM builtin allowlist and load child_process.
references:
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/19
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.007
  - attack.t1068
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_img:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\node.exe'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains|all:
      - 'Module._load'
      - 'child_process'
  condition: selection_img and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
  - Internal security testing reproducing the GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8 PoC
  - Legitimate Node internals tooling that calls Module._load with a child_process argument
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 (@Image:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe) OR @ParentImage:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe)) @CommandLine:*Module._load* @CommandLine:*child_process*
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 (ImageFileName=/\\node\.exe$/i OR ParentBaseFileName=/^node\.exe$/i)
| regex("Module\\._load", field=CommandLine, flags="i")
| regex("child_process", field=CommandLine, flags="i")
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, SHA256HashData], function=count())

[LLM] Vulnerable vm2 package present on managed assets (CVE-2026-43999) Reconnaissance High
Surfaces every device where Microsoft Defender Vulnerability Management has identified the vulnerable vm2 npm package vulnerable to CVE-2026-43999. Drives proactive remediation before the gadget UC1 ever needs to fire. Rationale: The advisory explicitly identifies the affected component (vm2 npm package, files `lib/builtin.js` at lines 40/86-90/109-117) and CVE-2026-43999 with CVSS 9.9. Vulnerability inventory is the highest-leverage detection surface for a library RCE because the exploit window is large. Sigma/Datadog/Falcon left empty because vulnerability inventory is not in their core EDR/log schemas — Defender TVM and Sentinel's mirrored table are the right path. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8

[LLM] node.exe spawning shell with discovery commands (vm2 post-exploit RCE indicator) Actions on Objectives Medium
Catches the post-exploitation step of CVE-2026-43999 where the attacker, having loaded `child_process` via `Module._load`, invokes `execSync` / `exec` to run discovery commands on the host — the PoC literally runs `cp.execSync('id')`. Surfaces node.exe parent → cmd/powershell/sh/bash child with classic recon arguments. Rationale: Targets the exact post-exploitation behaviour shown in the advisory's PoC (step 9: `cp.execSync('id')`). Pinning the parent to node.exe and the child to a shell with one of the article's discovery verbs keeps signal high while remaining article-specific — generic shell-spawn-from-Node would be too broad. Linux `id`/`whoami` are matched directly because they are the PoC's literal payload. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1059.003 T1059.004 T1033 T1082
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","sh","bash","dash","zsh","id","whoami","uname","hostname")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("whoami","uname","/etc/passwd","/etc/shadow","hostname","ipconfig","ifconfig","net user","net group","/proc/self"," id "," id;","id\n","id$")
   or FileName in~ ("id","whoami","uname")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessId, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc

(ImProcessCreate

title: Node.js Spawning Shell for Host Discovery (vm2 Sandbox Escape Post-Exploit, CVE-2026-43999)
id: 2d8f4b1e-6c5a-49b3-94d7-1f8e3c2a7b54
description: Detects node.exe spawning a shell process that immediately runs host-discovery commands (id, whoami, uname, hostname, /etc/passwd) — the post-exploitation pattern from the GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8 PoC where Module._load('child_process').execSync('id') runs on the host.
references:
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-947f-4v7f-x2v8
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/19
tags:
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.003
  - attack.t1059.004
  - attack.t1033
  - attack.t1082
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith: '\node.exe'

    Image|endswith:
      - '\cmd.exe'
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'whoami'
      - 'hostname'
      - 'ipconfig'
      - 'net user'
      - 'net group'
      - 'systeminfo'
      - 'uname'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
falsepositives:
  - Node-based CLI tools that legitimately shell out for host metadata (npm pre-install scripts, electron-builder)
  - Monitoring agents written in Node
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\\node.exe OR *\\Node.exe) @Image:(*\\cmd.exe OR *\\powershell.exe OR *\\pwsh.exe OR *\\PowerShell.exe) @CommandLine:(*whoami* OR *hostname* OR *ipconfig* OR "net user" OR "net group" OR *systeminfo* OR *uname*)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/^node(\.exe)?$/i
| ImageFileName=/\\(cmd|powershell|pwsh|sh|bash|zsh|dash)(\.exe)?$/i OR ImageFileName=/\/(id|whoami|uname|hostname)$/i
| regex("(?i)(whoami|uname|/etc/passwd|/etc/shadow|hostname|ipconfig|ifconfig|net\\s+user|net\\s+group|systeminfo|/proc/self|\\bid\\b)", field=CommandLine) OR ImageFileName=/\/(id|whoami|uname|hostname)$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

[LLM] Vulnerable vm2 deployment (CVE-2026-44005, 3.9.6–3.10.5) in software inventory Reconnaissance High
Surfaces hosts running vm2 versions vulnerable to the mutable-proxy host-prototype sandbox escape disclosed in GHSA-vwrp-x96c-mhwq. The article names a specific npm package and version range — exploitation requires the vulnerable bridge code to be loaded, so hunting attack surface in the TVM/SBOM is the highest-fidelity pre-exploit pivot. Rationale: The advisory explicitly names CVE-2026-44005 and the npm:vm2 vulnerable range >=3.9.6, <=3.10.5 patched in 3.11.0 — both pivots are taken directly from the article. TVM CveId match is exact; the SoftwareInventory regex catches versions before TVM has correlated them. Cross-checked with Tenable, GitLab advisory database, and Semgrep's coverage write-up. Cross-checked against: • https://www.tenable.com/cve/CVE-2026-44005 • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/vm2/CVE-2026-44005/ • https://github.com/patriksimek/vm2/security/advisories/GHSA-vwrp-x96c-mhwq • https://semgrep.dev/blog/2026/calling-back-to-vm2-and-escaping-sandbox/ • https://www.kodemsecurity.com/resources/vm2-sandbox-escape-vulnerabilities-the-2026-cve-wave-turning-ai-agents-into-host-rce-vectors
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007
Data sourcesVulnerabilities
// Pivot 1: TVM vulnerability table — exact CVE match
let TvmHits = DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
    | where CveId == "CVE-2026-44005"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, Source="TVM-CVE";
// Pivot 2: software inventory — catch installs TVM hasn't correlated yet (e.g. vendored inside other npm packages)
let InvHits = DeviceTvmSoftwareInventory
    | where SoftwareName =~ "vm2"
    | where SoftwareVersion matches regex @"^3\.(9\.([6-9]|1[0-9])|10\.[0-5])$"
    | extend CveId="CVE-2026-44005", VulnerabilitySeverityLevel="Critical", RecommendedSecurityUpdate="Upgrade vm2 >= 3.11.0", Source="SBOM"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, Source;
union TvmHits, InvHits
| summarize arg_max(Timestamp, *) by DeviceId, SoftwareVersion, Source
| order by Timestamp desc
// Sentinel surfaces the same TVM tables when M365D data connector is enabled
let TvmHits = DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where CveId == "CVE-2026-44005"
    | project TimeGenerated, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, Source="TVM-CVE";
let InvHits = DeviceInfo
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | extend AF = parse_json(AdditionalFields)
    | where tostring(AF) has "vm2"
    | extend SoftwareName="vm2", SoftwareVersion=tostring(AF.SoftwareVersion), CveId="CVE-2026-44005", VulnerabilitySeverityLevel="Critical", RecommendedSecurityUpdate="Upgrade vm2 >= 3.11.0", Source="DeviceInfo"
    | where SoftwareVersion matches regex @"^3\.(9\.([6-9]|1[0-9])|10\.[0-5])$"
    | project TimeGenerated, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor="npm", SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, Source;
union TvmHits, InvHits
| summarize arg_max(TimeGenerated, *) by DeviceId, SoftwareVersion, Source
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Node.js process spawning shell or LOLBin — vm2 sandbox-escape post-exploitation Exploitation Medium
Hunts the inevitable post-escape footprint of CVE-2026-44005: once sandboxed JS pollutes host Object.prototype/Function.prototype the attacker pivots to host RCE inside the node process, which manifests as node.exe (or `node` on Linux) spawning cmd/powershell/bash/scripting hosts/LOLBins. Article-specific because the named exploit primitive (host-prototype mutation) lands the attacker with arbitrary code execution inside the Node.js host realm, and child-process spawning is the cleanest cross-platform EDR signal of that escape. Rationale: The article's primitive (mutable proxies on host Object.prototype/Array.prototype/Function.prototype writable via otherReflectSet) lands the attacker with arbitrary code execution inside the host Node.js process — the canonical first observable step is spawning a child shell/LOLBin to broaden access. Article-specific because the trigger is literally `node.exe → cmd/powershell/bash/etc` chain; vanilla `Office → script-host` detections would miss it because the parent here is node. Cross-checked against Kodem's published IOCs and TheHackerWire's RCE write-ups which both list child-process spawning as the EDR-visible escape footprint. Cross-checked against: • https://www.kodemsecurity.com/resources/vm2-sandbox-escape-vulnerabilities-the-2026-cve-wave-turning-ai-agents-into-host-rce-vectors • https://thehackernews.com/2026/05/vm2-nodejs-library-vulnerabilities.html • https://socradar.io/blog/cve-2026-26956-vm2-sandbox-escape-rce-node-js-25/ • https://threataft.com/articles/vm2-nodejs-sandbox-escape-vulnerabilities-cve-2026
ATT&CKT1059 T1106 T1190

| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where AccountName !in~ ("system","local service","network service")
// strip the loud developer/build-toolchain noise
| where not (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("node-gyp","node_modules\\.bin\\","npm-cli.js","yarn.js","gatsby build","next build","webpack","vite build"))
| where not (InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_any (@"\node_modules\node-gyp\", @"\AppData\Local\Yarn\", @"\.npm\_cacache\", @"\GitHub Actions Runner\", @"\Jenkins\workspace\"))

description: Detects node.exe / node spawning interactive shells, script interpreters, or LOLBins — a high-fidelity post-exploitation signal for CVE-2026-44005 and the broader vm2 sandbox-escape family where sandboxed JS mutates host Object.prototype/Function.prototype and pivots to host RCE inside the Node.js process.

  category: process_creation

  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '\node.exe'

      - '\cmd.exe'
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
      - '\wscript.exe'
      - '\cscript.exe'
      - '\mshta.exe'
      - '\bash.exe'
      - '\wsl.exe'
      - '\certutil.exe'
      - '\bitsadmin.exe'
      - '\curl.exe'

      - 'node-gyp'
      - 'node_modules\.bin\'
      - 'yarn install'
      - 'npm run '
      - 'gatsby build'
      - 'next build'
      - 'webpack'

  - Developer toolchains invoking shell helpers from npm scripts (node-gyp, cross-spawn build steps)

[LLM] Vulnerable vm2 npm package (CVE-2026-43997, <=3.10.5) installed on host Exploitation Medium
Hunts for the creation/modification of a node_modules\vm2\package.json file on managed hosts, flagging deployments of the vm2 sandbox library whose <=3.10.5 versions are vulnerable to the HostObject sandbox escape. This is a vulnerable-attack-surface discovery hunt — pair the hit with a version inspection on the package.json to confirm exploitability. Rationale: The article identifies the affected package as `npm:vm2` with vuln range <= 3.10.5; node_modules\vm2\package.json is the canonical on-disk artefact of any vm2 installation and is the highest-fidelity asset-discovery signal short of querying SBOM/SCA. Cross-checked against NVD CVE-2026-43997, Bleeping Computer and Semgrep write-ups confirming versions and that the package name is unchanged. [LLM] Node.js spawning shell or recon binary (post-vm2-escape RCE) Actions on Objectives High
Alerts when node.exe spawns a Windows shell, LOLBin, or recon utility — the exact post-exploitation pattern produced by the CVE-2026-43997 PoC, which reaches `process.getBuiltinModule('child_process').execSync(...)` after escaping the vm2 sandbox. Production Node services have no legitimate reason to shell out to cmd/powershell/whoami/curl. Rationale: The article's PoC literally ends with `inspect.constructor("return process.getBuiltinModule('child_process').execSync('ls',{stdio:'inherit'})")();` — every successful exploitation of CVE-2026-43997 reaches child_process and on Windows that materialises as node.exe spawning cmd/powershell/LOLBin. The dev-tooling exclusion (npm/yarn/pnpm/node-gyp parents) preserves the post-install build pattern as a known FP without losing the production attack signal. Cross-checked with Kodem and Semgrep guidance that child_process spawning is the only reliable post-escape telemetry. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-47x8-96vw-5wg6 • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/critical-vm2-sandbox-bug-lets-attackers-execute-code-on-hosts/ • https://www.kodemsecurity.com/resources/vm2-sandbox-escape-vulnerabilities-the-2026-cve-wave-turning-ai-agents-into-host-rce-vectors • https://semgrep.dev/blog/2026/calling-back-to-vm2-and-escaping-sandbox/
ATT&CKT1059 T1059.003 T1059.001 T1106
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "node.exe"
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","sh.exe","bash.exe","wsl.exe","wmic.exe","whoami.exe","net.exe","net1.exe","ipconfig.exe","systeminfo.exe","tasklist.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe","nc.exe","ncat.exe")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessParentFileName !in~ ("npm.exe","npm.cmd","yarn.exe","yarn.cmd","pnpm.exe","pnpm.cmd","code.exe","electron.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName endswith "node.exe" or ParentProcessName =~ "node"
| extend ChildName = tostring(split(ActingProcessName, "\\")[-1])
| where ChildName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","sh.exe","bash.exe","wsl.exe","wmic.exe","whoami.exe","net.exe","net1.exe","ipconfig.exe","systeminfo.exe","tasklist.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe","certutil.exe","bitsadmin.exe","nc.exe","ncat.exe","sh","bash")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → [LLM] Vulnerable vm2 npm Package Deployment (CVE-2026-44006, <= 3.10.5) · Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Node.js Process Spawning Shell or System-Recon Binary (vm2 Sandbox Escape Post-Exploitation)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Vulnerable vm2 npm Package Deployment (CVE-2026-44006, <= 3.10.5) Reconnaissance Medium
Hunts hosts where the vm2 npm package is installed at a vulnerable version (<= 3.10.5). The article identifies a critical sandbox escape reachable via util.inspect → BaseHandler.getPrototypeOf — any service still bundling vm2 is RCE-exposed to any sandboxed user code, so inventory is the precondition for every other detection. Rationale: Article explicitly names the vulnerable package (npm:vm2 <= 3.10.5) and the exact file containing the bug (lib/bridge.js#L655-L658). File-event telemetry for these paths is the cleanest pre-attack inventory pivot. NVD/GitLab advisories confirm CVSS 10.0 and fix in 3.11.0. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/patriksimek/vm2/security/advisories/GHSA-qcp4-v2jj-fjx8 • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-44006 • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/vm2/CVE-2026-44006/
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// CVE-2026-44006 — locate vm2 deployments. Pair with `npm ls vm2` on hits to confirm version.
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FolderPath has @"node_modules\vm2" or FolderPath has "node_modules/vm2")
| where FileName =~ "package.json" or FileName =~ "main.js" or FileName =~ "bridge.js"
| summarize FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp), SampleFile = any(FolderPath), Writers = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName, 5) by DeviceId, DeviceName
| order by LastSeen desc
// CVE-2026-44006 — vm2 inventory via ASIM file-event telemetry (works for any vendor feeding ImFileEvent)
let LookbackDays = 30d;
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where (TargetFilePath has "node_modules/vm2" or TargetFilePath has @"node_modules\vm2")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("package.json","bridge.js","main.js")
| summarize FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated), SamplePath = any(TargetFilePath), Writers = make_set(ActingProcessName, 5) by DvcHostname
| order by LastSeen desc
