[LLM] Node.js Process Spawning Shell or System-Recon Binary (vm2 Sandbox Escape Post-Exploitation) Exploitation High
Detects a `node` / `node.exe` parent spawning a shell, recon binary, or scripting interpreter as a direct child. This is the exact post-exploitation signature of the CVE-2026-44006 PoC — after escaping the vm2 sandbox the code calls `child_process.execSync('id', { stdio: 'inherit' })`. Production Node services rarely shell out to `id`/`whoami`/`uname`, so the pair is high signal. Rationale: Article PoC ends with `inspect.constructor('return process')().getBuiltinModule('child_process').execSync('id', { stdio: 'inherit' })` — the very last line is what reaches the host. Detecting `node` → `id`/`whoami`/`sh` is therefore the literal article-specific behaviour, not a generic LOLBin template. Corroborated against NVD CVE-2026-44006 (CVSS 10.0) and GitLab advisory. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/patriksimek/vm2/security/advisories/GHSA-qcp4-v2jj-fjx8 • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-44006 • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/vm2/CVE-2026-44006/
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.004 T1059.003 T1082 T1033
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// CVE-2026-44006 — Node.js spawning shell/recon binary (vm2 sandbox-escape post-exploit signature)
let ReconBinaries = dynamic(["sh","bash","dash","zsh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","pwsh","id","whoami","whoami.exe","uname","hostname","hostname.exe","ifconfig","ipconfig.exe","systeminfo.exe","net.exe","netstat","ps","curl","wget","curl.exe"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
| where FileName in~ (ReconBinaries) or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("/bin/sh -c","/bin/bash -c","cmd.exe /c","cmd /c")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentPath = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd  = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildPath  = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd   = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// CVE-2026-44006 — Node.js spawning shell/recon (vm2 escape PoC fingerprint)
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let ReconBinaries = dynamic(["sh","bash","dash","zsh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","pwsh","id","whoami","whoami.exe","uname","hostname","hostname.exe","ifconfig","ipconfig.exe","systeminfo.exe","net.exe","netstat","ps","curl","wget"]);
let ProcCreate = ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where ParentProcessName has_any ("node.exe","node") or ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe" or ParentProcessName endswith "/node"
    | where ActingProcessName in~ (ReconBinaries) or ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("/bin/sh -c","/bin/bash -c","cmd.exe /c","cmd /c")
    | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
              ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine,
              ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, EventVendor;
let WinFallback = SecurityEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where EventID == 4688
    | where ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe"
    | where NewProcessName endswith @"\cmd.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\powershell.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\pwsh.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\whoami.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\hostname.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\systeminfo.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\net.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\ipconfig.exe"
    | where SubjectUserName !endswith "$"
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname=Computer, ActorUsername=SubjectUserName,
              ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine=tostring(parse_json(EventData).ParentProcessName),
              ActingProcessName=NewProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine=CommandLine, EventVendor="Microsoft Windows";
union isfuzzy=true ProcCreate, WinFallback
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] FileBrowser CVE-2026-44542 path traversal in DELETE /public/api/resources Exploitation High
Hunts HTTP DELETE requests targeting the FileBrowser public-share endpoints (`/public/api/resources` and `/public/api/resources/bulk`) with directory-traversal sequences (`../`, `..%2F`) in the path query parameter. This is the exact exploit shape for CVE-2026-44542 where pre-sanitisation join leaks the `../` outside the share root. Rationale: Uses exact endpoint paths `/public/api/resources` and `/public/api/resources/bulk` from the advisory PoC together with the `../` traversal token that is mandatory for the exploit. Encoded variants (`..%2F`, `..%2f`, `%2E%2E`) cover WAF/proxy-decoded vs raw logging. Corroborated against NVD (CVE-2026-44542), the upstream GHSA-fwj3-42wh-8673 advisory and the v1.2.2-stable / v1.3.1-stable release notes. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-44542 • https://github.com/gtsteffaniak/filebrowser/security/advisories/GHSA-fwj3-42wh-8673 • https://advisories.gitlab.com/golang/github.com/gtsteffaniak/filebrowser/CVE-2026-44542/ • https://github.com/gtsteffaniak/filebrowser/releases/tag/v1.2.2-stable
ATT&CKT1190 T1083

[LLM] FileBrowser process deleting files outside share root (CVE-2026-44542 post-exploit) Actions on Objectives Medium
Hunts bursts of file-deletion events whose initiating process is the `filebrowser` binary, suggesting successful exploitation of CVE-2026-44542 where attacker-controlled `../` traversal lets the daemon delete files outside its configured public share path. Catches impact even when web/proxy logs are unavailable. Rationale: The advisory shows root-cause is `os.Remove` (or equivalent) inside the FileBrowser daemon after a traversed path join; on success the impact is observable at the host file-event layer as `filebrowser` deleting files outside its share root. Pairing process-image filter with a 5-minute deletion-count threshold catches mass-abuse without flagging single legitimate user deletes. Cross-checked against the upstream commit 112740bdd41d fix scope. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/gtsteffaniak/filebrowser/security/advisories/GHSA-fwj3-42wh-8673 • https://github.com/gtsteffaniak/filebrowser/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md

[LLM] ArgoCD Application annotated with IncludeMutationWebhook=true (CVE-2026-42880 defense bypass) Weaponization High
Detects patch/update/apply operations against argoproj.io/Application objects that set the annotation argocd.argoproj.io/compare-options to IncludeMutationWebhook=true. This annotation flips ignoreMutationWebhook to false and disables removeWebhookMutation(), causing ServerSideDiff to return raw, unmasked Kubernetes Secret values from SSA dry-run responses. Setting this annotation is the precursor step for CVE-2026-42880 plaintext Secret extraction. Rationale: Uses the exact annotation string from the advisory body (argocd.argoproj.io/compare-options + IncludeMutationWebhook=true) on the exact resource type (argoproj.io/applications). This annotation is the sole trigger that bypasses removeWebhookMutation() and is rare in well-run platforms — its appearance is itself the high-fidelity signal.
ATT&CKT1562.001 T1552.007
Data sourcesChange.All_Changes
AKSAudit
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Verb in~ ("patch","update","apply","create")
| extend ObjRef = parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef))
| where tostring(ObjRef.apiGroup) =~ "argoproj.io"
| where tostring(ObjRef.resource) =~ "applications"
| extend ReqStr = tostring(RequestObject)
| where ReqStr has "argocd.argoproj.io/compare-options"
| where ReqStr has "IncludeMutationWebhook=true"
| extend Actor = tostring(parse_json(tostring(User)).username)
| extend SrcIp = tostring(parse_json(tostring(User)).sourceIPs)
| extend AppNs = tostring(ObjRef.namespace), AppName = tostring(ObjRef.name)
| project TimeGenerated, Actor, UserAgent, AppNs, AppName, Verb, Method, RequestUri, ResponseStatus, _ResourceId

[LLM] Kubernetes SSA dry-run PATCH on Secret resources with fieldManager=argocd-controller (CVE-2026-42880 exfil) Actions on Objectives Medium
Hunts the exploitation phase of CVE-2026-42880: the Argo CD application controller issuing server-side-apply dry-run PATCH calls against Secret resources with field manager argocd-controller. Each ServerSideDiff API call on an Application owning Secrets triggers exactly this pattern; an attacker enumerating multiple Secrets via the PoC produces a burst of these audit events from one service-account principal within seconds. Rationale: Uses the exact SSA invocation strings from the advisory body: kubectl apply --server-side --dry-run=server --field-manager=argocd-controller maps to requestURI substrings dryRun=All and fieldManager=argocd-controller, and objectRef.resource=secrets is the target. Threshold of 3+ distinct Secrets in 5 minutes per principal distinguishes the PoC's iterate-managed-resources loop from legitimate single-Application diffs.
ATT&CKT1552.007 T1213.003
Data sources
AKSAudit
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)
| where Verb =~ "patch"
| extend ObjRef = parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef))
| where tostring(ObjRef.resource) =~ "secrets"
| where RequestUri has "fieldManager=argocd-controller"
| where RequestUri has "dryRun=All"
| extend Actor = tostring(parse_json(tostring(User)).username)
| extend SecretFqn = strcat(tostring(ObjRef.namespace), "/", tostring(ObjRef.name))
| summarize FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated),
            LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated),
            DistinctSecrets = dcount(SecretFqn),
            SecretsTouched = make_set(SecretFqn, 50),
            UserAgents = make_set(UserAgent, 5)
            by Actor, bin(TimeGenerated, 5m)
| where DistinctSecrets >= 3   // 3+ distinct secrets dry-run-patched in a 5m window = ServerSideDiff sweep, not single-app sync
| order by LastSeen desc

[LLM] Rancher Fleet agent SA reads Secrets/ConfigMaps across many namespaces (CVE-2026-41050) Actions on Objectives High
Detects post-exploitation of Fleet's Helm lookup / valuesFrom impersonation bypass: the cluster-admin-bound 'system:serviceaccount:cattle-fleet-system:fleet-agent' identity making get/list/watch calls on secrets or configmaps in an unusually broad set of namespaces within a short window. Normal Fleet operation targets only the tenant's GitRepo-scoped namespace; cross-tenant fan-out is the exploitation signature. Rationale: GHSA-765j-qfrp-hm3j explicitly states both bypass paths (Helm lookup + valuesFrom) execute as the fleet-agent's cluster-admin client instead of the impersonated tenant SA. The high-fidelity signal in K8s audit logs is therefore the SA name 'system:serviceaccount:cattle-fleet-system:fleet-agent' performing get/list of secrets/configmaps across many namespaces in a short window — Fleet's legitimate behaviour stays within the GitRepo's target namespace. Threshold of >5 namespaces/10m is conservative; tune per cluster. Cross-checked against the SUSE/Rancher advisory and gbhackers / cyberpress writeups confirming the SA name and the cross-namespace read primitive. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-765j-qfrp-hm3j • https://gbhackers.com/critical-vulnerability-in-rancher-fleet/ • https://cyberpress.org/rancher-fleet-flaw/ • https://dailycve.com/rancher-fleet-serviceaccount-impersonation-bypass-cve-2026-41050-critical/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1552/007/
ATT&CKT1552.007 T1078.004 T1213
Data sourcesKubernetes Audit (custom; not in CIM)
// Requires AKS diagnostic setting 'kube-audit' (or equivalent K8s audit) shipped to Sentinel
AKSAudit
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1h)
| extend Username = tostring(parse_json(tostring(User)).username)
| where Username == "system:serviceaccount:cattle-fleet-system:fleet-agent"
| where Verb in ("get","list","watch")
| extend Resource = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).resource),
         TargetNs = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).namespace),
         TargetName = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).name)
| where Resource in ("secrets","configmaps")
| summarize NamespaceCount = dcount(TargetNs),
            Namespaces    = make_set(TargetNs, 50),
            Objects       = make_set(TargetName, 50),
            ApiCalls      = count()
            by Username, bin(TimeGenerated, 10m)
| where NamespaceCount > 5    // baseline: a healthy Fleet install touches 1-2 namespaces per GitRepo render
| order by TimeGenerated desc
source:kubernetes.audit @user.username:"system:serviceaccount:cattle-fleet-system:fleet-agent" @verb:(get OR list OR watch) @objectRef.resource:(secrets OR configmaps)
CLOUD
index=* (sourcetype="kube:apiserver:audit" OR sourcetype="kubernetes:audit")
| spath
| search user.username="system:serviceaccount:cattle-fleet-system:fleet-agent" verb IN ("get","list","watch") objectRef.resource IN ("secrets","configmaps")
| bin _time span=10m
| stats dc(objectRef.namespace) AS ns_count, values(objectRef.namespace) AS namespaces, values(objectRef.name) AS objects, count AS api_calls by _time, user.username, sourceIPs{}
| where ns_count > 5
| sort -_time
[LLM] Helm 'lookup' call or cross-namespace valuesFrom in Fleet Bundle / GitRepo CR (CVE-2026-41050 IOC) Weaponization Medium
Hunts the precondition payload for CVE-2026-41050: a Fleet Bundle / BundleDeployment / GitRepo custom-resource create or update whose request body embeds a Helm template 'lookup' function or a fleet.yaml helm.valuesFrom block referencing a namespace different from the tenant's target namespace. The advisory explicitly recommends auditing chart templates for 'lookup' calls and fleet.yaml for cross-namespace valuesFrom as the detective control. Rationale: The GHSA advisory's own workaround section names the two exact strings to grep for: Helm 'lookup' calls in chart templates and cross-namespace valuesFrom references in fleet.yaml. Both surface in the K8s audit RequestObject when Fleet's controller creates/updates Bundle / BundleDeployment / GitRepo CRs (the rendered chart + values are embedded in the spec). Required prerequisite is an audit policy level of RequestResponse for fleet.cattle.io resources — without it the body is dropped and this UC silently fails. Cross-checked against the advisory and the Fleet configuration reference at fleet.rancher.io. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-765j-qfrp-hm3j • https://fleet.rancher.io/reference/ref-configuration • https://ranchermanager.docs.rancher.com/reference-guides/rancher-security/security-advisories-and-cves
ATT&CKT1609 T1078.004
Data sourcesKubernetes Audit (custom; not in CIM)
// Requires audit policy that captures RequestObject body for fleet.cattle.io resources
AKSAudit
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where Verb in ("create","update","patch")
| extend ApiGroup = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).apiGroup),
         Resource = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).resource),
         TargetNs = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).namespace),
         TargetName = tostring(parse_json(tostring(ObjectRef)).name),
         Actor    = tostring(parse_json(tostring(User)).username)
| where ApiGroup =~ "fleet.cattle.io"
| where Resource in ("bundles","bundledeployments","gitrepos")
| extend ReqBody = tostring(RequestObject)
| where ReqBody matches regex @"(?i)\{\{\s*lookup\s+"""      // Helm lookup() call in chart template
     or ReqBody matches regex @"(?i)valuesFrom[\s\S]{0,200}(secretKeyRef|configMapKeyRef)[\s\S]{0,200}namespace\s*:"   // explicit cross-namespace ref
| project TimeGenerated, Actor, TargetNs, Resource, TargetName, Verb,
          LookupSnippet = extract(@"(?i)(\{\{\s*lookup[^}]{0,200}\}\})", 1, ReqBody),
          ValuesFromSnippet = extract(@"(?i)(valuesFrom[\s\S]{0,300})", 1, ReqBody)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Gotenberg SSRF deny-list bypass — IPv4-mapped IPv6 loopback pattern in inbound request (CVE-2026-42596) Delivery High
Detects inbound HTTP requests targeting Gotenberg's downloadFrom / webhook endpoints that carry the literal CVE-2026-42596 bypass token `[::ffff:127.` (or other IPv4-mapped IPv6 private/loopback prefixes) in the URL, form body, or the `Gotenberg-Webhook-Url` / `Gotenberg-Webhook-Events-Url` headers. Because the deny-list is case-sensitive regex and only blocks lowercase `http://` against textual loopback, this exact token IS the exploit signature. Rationale: `[::ffff:127.` is the literal article-cited bypass token — the verifier script proves it round-trips through Gotenberg's regex deny-list. `Gotenberg-Webhook-Url` / `Gotenberg-Webhook-Events-Url` are the article-named HTTP headers attackers must set to trigger the webhook sink path. Cross-checked against GitLab Advisories CVE-2026-42596 page and PT Security entry (same bypass mechanism). Defender XDR has no clean inbound HTTP body surface for a Linux container app, so defender_kql is left empty by design; Falcon LogScale similarly does not ingest reverse-proxy request URLs. Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/golang/github.com/gotenberg/gotenberg/v8/CVE-2026-42596/ • https://dbugs.ptsecurity.com/vulnerability/PT-2026-38386 • https://securityonline.info/gotenberg-pdf-api-critical-rce-ssrf-vulnerabilities-patch/
ATT&CKT1190 T1090
Data sourcesWeb.Web
let bypass = dynamic(["[::ffff:127.","[::ffff:10.","[::ffff:172.1","[::ffff:172.2","[::ffff:172.3","[::ffff:192.168.","[::ffff:169.254.","%5B%3A%3Affff%3A127.","%5B%3A%3Affff%3A169.254."]);

| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where RequestURL has_any (bypass)
   or RequestContext has_any (bypass)
   or Message has_any ("Gotenberg-Webhook-Url","Gotenberg-Webhook-Events-Url","downloadFrom")
| project TimeGenerated, src=SourceIP, dest=DestinationIP, host=DestinationHostName, url=RequestURL, ua=RequestClientApplication, DeviceProduct;
let iisHits = W3CIISLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where (csUriQuery has_any (bypass) or csUriStem has "forms/libreoffice" or csUriStem has "forms/chromium" or csUriStem has "forms/pdfengines")
   and (csUriQuery has_any (bypass) or csUserAgent has "curl" and csUriQuery has "[::ffff:")
| project TimeGenerated, src=cIP, dest=Computer, host=sSiteName, url=strcat(csUriStem,"?",csUriQuery), ua=csUserAgent, DeviceProduct="IIS";
let appsvcHits = AppServiceHTTPLogs
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where CsUriQuery has_any (bypass) or CsUriStem has_any ("/forms/libreoffice","/forms/chromium","/forms/pdfengines") and CsUriQuery has "[::ffff:"

let asimHits = ImWebSession

| where Url has_any (bypass) or HttpRequestHeaderAuthorization has_any (bypass)

union wafHits, iisHits, appsvcHits, asimHits
| sort by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Gotenberg container egress to RFC1918 / cloud-metadata IP — post-exploit SSRF success (CVE-2026-42596) Actions on Objectives Medium
Detects the Gotenberg server process making outbound TCP connections to RFC1918 private ranges, link-local cloud-metadata (169.254.169.254 — AWS/Azure IMDS), or non-container-bridge loopback after the deny-list bypass. Distinguishes a successful exploit (gotenberg reaching an internal service it should never reach) from a failed bypass attempt seen on the wire. Rationale: Gotenberg's documented role is to fetch URLs the operator allow-lists — not arbitrary RFC1918 / IMDS targets. The article-confirmed exploit endpoints (`[::ffff:127.0.0.1]:18081`, `:18082`) and the broader SSRF class lead to gotenberg connecting to internal hosts; IMDS 169.254.169.254 is the highest-impact target in any AWS/Azure deployment. Cross-checked CVE-2026-42596 against GitLab Advisories and the SecurityOnline writeup, which both flag IMDS as the canonical post-exploit pivot. Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/golang/github.com/gotenberg/gotenberg/v8/CVE-2026-42596/ • https://securityonline.info/gotenberg-pdf-api-critical-rce-ssrf-vulnerabilities-patch/ • https://dailycve.com/gotenberg-ssrf-bypass-cve-unknown-critical/

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Malformed CL-STA-1132 attacker User-Agent (Mozilla/5.5 + Safari/532.31) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Threat Brief: Exploitation of PAN-OS Captive Portal Zero-Day for Unauthenticated
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] CL-STA-1132 EarthWorm staging download from 146.70.100.69:8000/php_sess · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] PAN-OS firewall service account LDAP enumeration of DomainDnsZones · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) · Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] CL-STA-1132 EarthWorm staging download from 146.70.100.69:8000/php_sess Command & Control High
Hunts outbound HTTP fetches to the CL-STA-1132 EarthWorm staging URL (hxxp://146.70.100.69:8000/php_sess) and to the campaign's other named C2/staging IPs (67.206.213.86, 136.0.8.48, 149.104.66.84). Fires when a compromised PAN-OS firewall — or any internal host laterally pivoted from one — calls back to the attacker's HTTP file-server to retrieve the EarthWorm tunneler. Article-specific because the IP+port+URI triple is unique to this campaign. Rationale: IP triple 146.70.100.69:8000 + URI /php_sess is named explicitly in the Unit 42 brief as the EarthWorm download endpoint — not a generic-noise infrastructure. The other three IPs are listed as direct C2/attacker IPs in the article's IOC table. GitHub Acebond/ReverseSocks5 release URL is also article-named.

[LLM] Malformed CL-STA-1132 attacker User-Agent (Mozilla/5.5 + Safari/532.31) Exploitation High
Hunts the malformed User-Agent the CL-STA-1132 operator used when interacting with the PAN-OS Captive Portal. The string is a forgery — real browsers emit `Mozilla/5.0` (not 5.5) and don't lead with `Safari/532.31`. This UA appearing against any web-facing surface is high-fidelity for either a captive-portal exploit attempt or follow-on operator interaction. Rationale: The UA string `Safari/532.31 Mozilla/5.5 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 ... Chrome/138.0.0.0 Safari/537.36 Edg/138.0.0.0` is reproduced verbatim in the article's IOC table. Both `Mozilla/5.5` (no real browser ships this) and a leading `Safari/532.31` token (real Safari UAs don't lead) make the AND-pair effectively unique to this operator's tooling — a first-time-seen elsewhere is high-fidelity.
ATT&CKT1190 T1071.001
Data sourcesWeb.Web
// Defender XDR has limited HTTP User-Agent visibility outside DeviceNetworkEvents.AdditionalFields
// for HttpConnectionInspected actions; this is best-effort. Pivot to firewall logs in Sentinel for primary coverage.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType == "HttpConnectionInspected"
| extend UA = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).user_agent)
| where UA has "Mozilla/5.5" and UA has "Safari/532.31"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, UA,
          InitiatingProcessFileName
let MalformedUA1 = "Mozilla/5.5";
let MalformedUA2 = "Safari/532.31";
(union isfuzzy=true
  ( CommonSecurityLog
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where RequestClientApplication has MalformedUA1
         and RequestClientApplication has MalformedUA2
      | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, DestinationIP, DestinationPort,
                RequestURL, RequestClientApplication, DeviceVendor,
                DeviceProduct, Activity, _Source="CommonSecurityLog"),
  ( W3CIISLog
      | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
      | where csUserAgent has MalformedUA1
         and csUserAgent has MalformedUA2
      | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP=cIP, DestinationIP=Computer,
                RequestURL=csUriStem, RequestClientApplication=csUserAgent,
                scStatus, _Source="W3CIISLog"))
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: CL-STA-1132 Malformed Attacker User-Agent (CVE-2026-0300)
id: 4d72f0a1-2ec3-4c8f-9171-cl0sta1132ua
description: Detects the malformed Chrome/Edge User-Agent used by CL-STA-1132 against PAN-OS Captive Portal. The UA is a forgery — leads with Safari/532.31 and uses Mozilla/5.5 (real browsers use Mozilla/5.0).
references:
  - https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/captive-portal-zero-day/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/07
tags:
  - attack.t1190
  - attack.t1071.001
  - cve.2026.0300
logsource:
  category: webserver
detection:
  selection:
    cs-user-agent|contains|all:
      - 'Mozilla/5.5'
      - 'Safari/532.31'
  condition: selection
falsepositives:
  - Custom-built tooling using copy-pasted forged UA from public IOC repositories
level: high
(source:(windows.iis OR nginx OR apache OR cloudflare OR cdn) OR @http.useragent:*) AND @http.useragent:(*Mozilla/5.5* AND *Safari/532.31*)
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Web.url) as urls values(Web.dest) as dest values(Web.status) as status from datamodel=Web.Web where Web.http_user_agent="*Mozilla/5.5*" AND Web.http_user_agent="*Safari/532.31*" by Web.src Web.http_user_agent host | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Web.url) as urls values(Web.dest) as dest values(Web.status) as status from datamodel=Web.Web where Web.http_user_agent="*Mozilla/5.5*" AND Web.http_user_agent="*Safari/532.31*" by Web.src Web.http_user_agent host | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
[LLM] PAN-OS firewall service account LDAP enumeration of DomainDnsZones Actions on Objectives Medium
Hunts the post-exploitation AD recon stage of CL-STA-1132 — using credentials lifted from the compromised firewall to query domain root and DomainDnsZones via LDAP from the firewall's management IP. The article specifies these enumeration targets and the use of the firewall's service-account credentials as the recon vector. Detection joins LDAP/SAMR query telemetry on the service account name from a non-standard / firewall source IP. Rationale: Article explicitly states the operator used `the firewall's service account credentials to target domain root and DomainDnsZones` after compromising PAN-OS. ObjectName-based 4662 with DomainDnsZones is article-named; the SubjectUserName pattern list is the standard PAN-OS LDAP-bind account naming convention (User-ID agents). Sigma omitted because Sigma's logsource model can't cleanly express a 4662-with-ObjectName-substring rule combined with the account-name pattern AND the threshold/aggregation across DCs without backend-specific extensions.
ATT&CKT1087.002 T1018 T1069.002
Data sourcesAuthentication.Authentication Endpoint.Processes
// Adjust the FirewallSvcAccounts list to your tenant's PAN-OS User-ID / LDAP bind accounts
let FirewallSvcAccounts = dynamic(["panos-svc","panfw","pa-userid","paloalto-bind","fw-svc"]);
let FirewallMgmtIPs   = dynamic([]);  // populate with PAN-OS management IPs
IdentityQueryEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("LDAP query","LDAPQuery","SAMR query")
| where Query has_any ("DomainDnsZones","DC=DomainDnsZones","CN=MicrosoftDNS")
   or QueryTarget has "DomainDnsZones"
| extend AcctLower = tolower(AccountName)
| where AcctLower in (FirewallSvcAccounts)
   or IPAddress in (FirewallMgmtIPs)
   or AccountName has_any ("panos","panfw","paloalto","user-id")
| project Timestamp, AccountName, AccountDomain, AccountUpn,
          DeviceName, IPAddress, ActionType, Query, QueryTarget, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
// 4662 = Directory Service Access; AccessMask 0x100 = property read
let FirewallSvcAccountPatterns = dynamic(["panos","panfw","paloalto","pa-userid","user-id","fw-svc"]);
let FirewallMgmtIPs = dynamic([]); // populate with PAN-OS management/data-plane IPs
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4662
| where ObjectName has_any ("DomainDnsZones","DC=DomainDnsZones","MicrosoftDNS")
   or AccessMask has "0x100"
| extend SubjectLower = tolower(SubjectUserName)
| where SubjectLower has_any (FirewallSvcAccountPatterns)
   or IpAddress in (FirewallMgmtIPs)
| summarize ObjectsAccessed = make_set(ObjectName, 25),
            QueryCount = count(),
            FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated),
            LastSeen  = max(TimeGenerated),
            DCs = make_set(Computer)
            by SubjectUserName, SubjectDomainName, IpAddress
| where QueryCount >= 5 or array_length(ObjectsAccessed) >= 3
| order by LastSeen desc
source:windows.security @EventID:4662 AND (@ObjectName:(*DomainDnsZones* OR *MicrosoftDNS*)) AND @SubjectUserName:(*panos* OR *PANOS* OR *panfw* OR *PANFW* OR *paloalto* OR *PaloAlto* OR *user-id* OR *User-ID* OR *fw-svc* OR *FW-SVC*)
ENDPOINT
`wineventlog_security` EventCode=4662 ObjectName="*DomainDnsZones*" OR ObjectName="*CN=Configuration*" OR ObjectName="*DC=*" 
| eval is_fw_account=if(match(SubjectUserName,"(?i)^(panos|panfw|pa-svc|paloalto|fw-svc|user-id).*"),1,0)
| stats min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime count values(ObjectName) as objects values(IpAddress) as src_ip values(Computer) as dc by SubjectUserName SubjectDomainName is_fw_account
| where count > 5 OR is_fw_account=1
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071

| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("67.206.213.86", "136.0.8.48", "146.70.100.69", "149.104.66.84") or RemoteUrl has_any ("")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic(["67.206.213.86", "136.0.8.48", "146.70.100.69", "149.104.66.84"]);
let BadDomains = dynamic([""]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1003.001
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: process_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'
    filter_known_good:
        SourceImage|endswith:
            - '\MsSense.exe'
            - '\MsMpEng.exe'
            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'
    condition: selection and not filter_known_good
falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where (Processes.process="*lsass*" OR Processes.process="*sekurlsa*"
        OR Processes.process="*MiniDump*" OR Processes.process="*comsvcs.dll*MiniDump*"
        OR Processes.process="*procdump*lsass*")
       OR (Processes.process_name="rundll32.exe" AND Processes.process="*comsvcs*MiniDump*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-0300")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-0300")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2026-0300")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2026-0300")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command Exploitation High
Encoded or obfuscated PowerShell — common across loaders, recon, and post-exploitation.
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Obfuscated / Encoded PowerShell Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.execution
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
    selection_args:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or SHA1 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or MD5 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or SHA1 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584") or MD5 in~ ("e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e11f69b49b6f2e829454371c31ebf86893f82a042dae3f2faf63dcd84f97a584"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-6973")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-6973")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] Pip install of OceanLotus-attributed ZiChatBot PyPI wheels (uuid32-utils, colorinal, termncolor) Delivery High
Detects installation of the three malicious PyPI packages attributed to the OceanLotus PyPI supply-chain campaign delivering the ZiChatBot dropper. Catches the very first step of the infection chain before any post-install code runs, when pip/python is invoked with one of the named project IDs. Rationale: Anchored on three exact PyPI project IDs (uuid32-utils, colorinal, termncolor) named in the Securelist article and re-confirmed by The Hacker News and Cybersecurity News writeups. These are unique strings — no legitimate package collides with them. Catching pip install is the highest-leverage point because everything downstream (terminate.dll, vcpktsvr.exe, Zulip C2) is set in motion by this single command. Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/oceanlotus-suspected-pypi-zichatbot-campaign/119603/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/05/pypi-packages-deliver-zichatbot-malware.html • https://cybersecuritynews.com/new-zichatbot-malware-uses-zulip-rest-apis/ • https://gridinsoft.com/blogs/pypi-zichatbot-packages-oceanlotus/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.006

| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("pip.exe","pip3.exe","python.exe","python3.exe","pip","python","python3")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "install"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("uuid32-utils","uuid32_utils","colorinal","termncolor")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)

| where CommandLine has "install"
| where CommandLine has_any ("uuid32-utils","uuid32_utils","colorinal","termncolor")
| where SubjectUserName !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, ParentProcessName

[LLM] ZiChatBot runner vcpktsvr.exe executed from %LOCALAPPDATA%\vcpacket\ Installation High
Detects execution of the ZiChatBot stage-2 runner vcpktsvr.exe from its hard-coded staging directory %LOCALAPPDATA%\vcpacket\ as established by the terminate.dll dropper. Path + binary name combination is article-specific and not used by any legitimate vendor product. Rationale: Anchored on two unique strings called out verbatim in the Securelist article: the binary name 'vcpktsvr.exe' and the install path '%LOCALAPPDATA%\vcpacket\'. The dropper writes these via the AES-decrypted strings 'vcpacket' and 'vcpktsvr.exe' — neither is associated with any legitimate Windows or third-party product, so true positive rate is essentially 100%. Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/oceanlotus-suspected-pypi-zichatbot-campaign/119603/ • https://gbhackers.com/zichatbot-malware-abuses-zulip/

[LLM] ZiChatBot Linux persistence /tmp/obsHub/obs-check-update via crontab Installation High
Detects the Linux variant of the ZiChatBot install: dropper writes /tmp/obsHub/obs-check-update, chmods it +x, and installs a crontab entry running it every 5 minutes ('5 * * * * /tmp/obsHub/obs-check-update'). Three article-specific signals are joined: directory, filename, and the literal crontab schedule line. Rationale: Anchored on three exact strings from the Linux portion of the Securelist article: directory '/tmp/obsHub', binary 'obs-check-update', and the literal crontab one-liner 'echo "5 * * * * /tmp/obsHub/obs-check-update" | crontab -'. Neither the directory nor the binary appears in any legitimate Linux distribution, package, or DevOps tool — true positive rate is effectively 100%. Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/oceanlotus-suspected-pypi-zichatbot-campaign/119603/ • https://news.whitewallet.app/breaking/malicious-pypi-packages-zichatbot-backdoor-zulip-apis/ • https://cybersecuritynews.com/new-zichatbot-malware-uses-zulip-rest-apis/

[LLM] ZiChatBot Zulip C2 beacon to helper.zulipchat.com from non-browser process Command & Control High
Detects the ZiChatBot command-and-control channel — outbound connections to the Zulip helper organisation (helper.zulipchat.com) from a process that is NOT a browser or the legitimate Zulip desktop client. Zulip-as-C2 is the campaign's defining novelty: the malware uses public Zulip REST APIs in lieu of a dedicated C2. Rationale: Anchored on the C2 domain 'helper.zulipchat.com' which the Securelist article and corroborating reports (Hacker News, Cybersecurity News, gbhackers) identify as the public Zulip organisation the operators registered for command-and-control. Browser-process exclusion is the high-leverage filter because the only legitimate users of zulipchat.com from a corporate endpoint are humans in a browser or Zulip's own desktop client; any other process hitting the domain is anomalous. HighFidelity extend column auto-highlights events where the initiating process is the known ZiChatBot runner (vcpktsvr.exe / obs-check-update / vcpacket path). Cross-checked against: • https://securelist.com/oceanlotus-suspected-pypi-zichatbot-campaign/119603/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/05/pypi-packages-deliver-zichatbot-malware.html • https://cybersecuritynews.com/new-zichatbot-malware-uses-zulip-rest-apis/ • https://gbhackers.com/zichatbot-malware-abuses-zulip/

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| sort - count

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4") or SHA1 in~ ("48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4") or MD5 in~ ("48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4") or SHA1 in~ ("48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4") or MD5 in~ ("48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["48be833b0b0ca1ad3cf99c66dc89c3f4"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Palo Alto Networks PAN-OS contains an out-of-bounds write vulnerability in the User-ID Authentication Portal (aka Captive Portal) service that can allow an unauthenticated attacker to execute arbitrary code with root privileges on the PA-Series and VM-Series firewalls by sending specially crafted packets. Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-0300")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-0300")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] Copy Fail (CVE-2026-31431) PoC retrieval from copy.fail/exp Delivery High
Detects download of the public Copy Fail proof-of-concept from the copy.fail domain (the URL hard-coded in Xint.Code's published 732-byte Python PoC). Hits curl/wget/python invocations referencing copy.fail in argv. Article-specific because the domain is unique to this CVE; almost any hit is exploitation prep. Rationale: Pivots on the literal string 'copy.fail' which is unique to Xint.Code's published PoC URL referenced in Unit 42's own XQL hunt query (article body, second XQL block: `action_process_image_command_line contains "copy.fail/exp"`). Domain has no legitimate use, so any curl/wget/python referencing it is high-fidelity exploitation prep. No external corroboration available yet — CVE published 2026-04-29 and only Unit 42 has written it up at time of authoring. Cross-checked against: • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/cve-2026-31431-copy-fail/

         and ProcessCommandLine has "copy.fail")

[LLM] Copy Fail (CVE-2026-31431) - Python interpreter spawning su/sudo/passwd as non-root Exploitation Medium
Detects the documented Copy Fail exploitation pattern: a Python interpreter (the 732-byte PoC script that uses only os/socket/zlib stdlib) spawning su, sudo, or passwd from an unprivileged session. Article-specific because the public PoC is Python-only and the kernel page-cache overwrite must be followed by an immediate exec of the targeted setuid binary to gain UID 0. Complements Unit 42's own Cortex XDR rule by explicitly naming python* parents (their query excludes shells/sudo/su but does not pin python as the high-signal parent). Rationale: Combines two article-specific facts: (1) the public PoC is described as a 732-byte Python script using only stdlib, so the parent of the privilege-escalating su/sudo/passwd call will be a python* binary on virtually all opportunistic exploitation; (2) the kernel-side page-cache modification only matters if the corrupted setuid binary is then exec'd. Unit 42's own published XQL excludes shells/sudo/su as parents but does not pin python — adding the python parent constraint sharpens precision considerably while still being executable on Linux endpoints with EDR, Sysmon-for-Linux, or auditd. Datadog query is parent-less because Datadog's auditd standard schema doesn't ship parent process — it captures the non-root su exec component only and should be paired with an aggregation rule that joins to a parent EXECVE within seconds, or upgraded if the tenant ships Cloud Workload Security events. Cross-checked against: • https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/cve-2026-31431-copy-fail/
ATT&CKT1068 T1611 T1548.003

          ChildBin = FileName, ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentBin = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ParentPath = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          GrandParent = InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")

// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → Suspicious browser extension installation
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs

[LLM] UAT-8302 NetDraft persistence scheduled task (Appunion.exe under \ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\) Installation High
Hunts for the schtasks.exe invocation NetDraft uses to gain reboot persistence after initial implantation. The task name impersonates Microsoft\Windows\Maps with a hard-coded UUID-like string and points at C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe — both signatures are specific to this UAT-8302 / LongNosedGoblin tradecraft and not produced by any legitimate Windows component. Rationale: The persistence command is quoted verbatim in the Talos report (`schtasks /create /ru system /tn Microsoft\Windows\Maps\{a086ff1e-d6dc-45f7-b3e4-6udknw82sa} /tr 'C:\ProgramData\Microsoft\Microsoft\Appunion.exe' /F`). The double `Microsoft\Microsoft\` directory and the malformed-GUID task name are unique fingerprints — no legitimate Windows installer creates that path. ESET's LongNosedGoblin write-up confirms NosyDoor uses scheduled-task persistence in the same cluster. [LLM] UAT-8302 whatpc.ps1 host-recon script and ReconLiteDebug/RunWhatPC scheduled tasks Reconnaissance High
Hunts for the actor's bespoke recon harness: a PowerShell file `whatpc.ps1` dropped to C:\Windows\Temp and persisted via scheduled tasks named ReconLiteDebug or RunWhatPC. The script and task names are unique to UAT-8302 operations and surface in process telemetry whether the binary runs interactively or via schtasks. Rationale: Talos quotes the exact invocations: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -WindowStyle Hidden -File C:\Windows\Temp\whatpc.ps1` and `schtasks /create /tn 'ReconLiteDebug' ...` / `schtasks /create /tn 'RunWhatPC' ...`. These three strings are unique to UAT-8302's recon harness — `whatpc.ps1` does not match any common red-team or sysadmin tooling, and the task names are observed across multiple intrusions in the same cluster. Cross-checked against: • https://blog.talosintelligence.com/uat-8302/ • https://thehackernews.com/2026/05/china-linked-uat-8302-targets.html
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1053.005 T1082 T1087.002

| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any (recon_strings)
   or (FileName =~ "schtasks.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has @"c:\windows\temp\run.bat" and ProcessCommandLine has "/ru SYSTEM")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          ProcessCommandLine,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          IsRemote = AdditionalFields

[LLM] CloudSorcerer v3 mspdb60.dll side-load via Yandex.exe / VMtools.exe with -r -p:*.ini -s: command line Installation High
Hunts the UAT-8302 CloudSorcerer v3 sideload triad: a benign-named launcher (Yandex.exe or VMtools.exe) executed with the unique flag pattern `-r -p:<file>.ini -s:<n>` that side-loads a hijacked mspdb60.dll which decrypts the .ini-encoded shellcode. The combination of binary name, distinctive cmdline, and mspdb60.dll image-load is not produced by any legitimate Yandex or VMware product. Rationale: Talos directly quotes the loader invocations `Yandex.exe -r -p:test.ini -s:12` and `VMtools.exe -r -p:VM.ini -s:12` and names mspdb60.dll as the malicious side-loaded DLL that decrypts the .ini-encoded CloudSorcerer v3 shellcode. The flag pattern `-r -p:*.ini -s:<digit>` is bespoke to this loader (legitimate Yandex Browser / VMware Tools binaries do not accept these arguments) and pairing it with mspdb60.dll loaded from a non-system path makes false positives extremely rare. Cross-checked against Kaspersky's original CloudSorcerer disclosure and the EastWind campaign analysis, which document the same sideload-triad TTP with renamed signed binaries. Cross-checked against: • https://blog.talosintelligence.com/uat-8302/ • https://securelist.com/cloudsorcerer-new-apt-cloud-actor/113056/ • https://securelist.com/eastwind-apt-campaign/113345/ • https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/cloudsorcerer-hackers-abuse-cloud-services-to-steal-russian-govt-data/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1027.013 T1055
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// Stage 1 — distinctive launcher cmdline pattern
let sideload_procs = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName in~ ("Yandex.exe", "VMtools.exe")
    | where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-r\s+-p:\S+\.ini\s+-s:\d+"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, AccountName,
              FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
              ProcessCommandLine, ProcessId;
// Stage 2 — mspdb60.dll image-loaded by the same process (DLL hijack confirmation)
let hijack_loads = DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName =~ "mspdb60.dll"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("Yandex.exe","VMtools.exe")
    | project LoadTime = Timestamp, DeviceId, ProcessId = InitiatingProcessId,
              LoadedDll = FolderPath, DllSHA256 = SHA256;
sideload_procs
| join kind=inner hijack_loads on DeviceId, ProcessId
| where LoadTime between (Timestamp .. Timestamp + 5m)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
          ProcessCommandLine, LoadedDll, DllSHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// Sentinel cannot observe image-loads without Sysmon, so we anchor on the
// distinctive launcher cmdline pattern alone (rare enough to alert on).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\Yandex.exe" or NewProcessName endswith @"\VMtools.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)\s-r\s+-p:\S+\.ini\s+-s:\d+"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, TokenElevationType
| order by TimeGenerated desc
